## waypoints

1. **W1007** (ready) The open-commitment count per repo, from mtools' commitment ledger, is a ratchet on the pace board
   and gauges
   - next: When mtools publishes its commitment ledger, count each repo's open commitments into a series, judge it like
     a file ratchet, and publish it on the pace board and gauges; the gate refusal reads it
2. **W1008** (ready) A shape warns on an open commitment that names a card of this repo whose evidence is no newer than
   the statement
   - next: When mtools publishes its commitment ledger, read each open commitment that names a card of this repo into
     the graph, and warn when the card's evidence is no newer than the statement
3. **W1075** (ready) Take an idle-host staging figure for one test target and split it by input (venv, Chromium, opa,
   Codon, corpus)
4. **W1139** (ready) The pace carries cards and landing-rate rows beside the ratchets, each with its horizon in days at
   the landing rate
   - next: The cards and landing-rate rows: read how the per-card series (W1138) and the pace rows consume weekly(),
     then add the cards-opened/closed and landing-rate columns for a queue with no git history, reading the stamps-only
     snapshots; test on the same queue outside a repository
5. **W1279** (ready) Pay down DOC201 in src: a Returns section written from each function body, in slices of about 12
   sites on files no neighbour slice shares
   - next: Slice 2 (during stack 679's gate): the next files alphabetically, adapter.py (6 sites), apischema.py (5),
     cachestore.py (3) = 14 sites, none shared with 679 (alarm, mspdi, pins, projection); read each function body first,
     write Returns from it, keep first lines, measure without --write while a stack carries ruff-baseline.json, census
     once after the measure.
6. **W913** (ready) A nemik witness that is already true when its card is minted is refused, and a witness may carry a
   freshness bound
   - next: Design first: at --add a witness is evaluated once and a true verdict is a defect (a check in nemik-check for
     a ready or blocked witnessed card whose witness holds on today's facts); then an optional fresh_since bound. Read
     witnesses.open_witnesses and the W59 check before editing
7. **W1096** (ready) Trace the 370 MiB the Model rebuild holds outside the graph: findings, annotation indices, SHACL
   caches, parsed JSON, the document
   - next: After stack521 lands and ships: read the pod's nemik_build_peak_rss_bytes and cgroup peaks for the new image;
     then the remaining levers by size: the survey's own merged graph and Model's merge (still two), pyshacl's clone per
     validation, the Activity triples (W1095, 196 MiB)

   - next: The route is documented; remaining: the first send is a deliberate step of its own: build the send behind an

     test with a recorded gh api fixture, then ask for an explicit go before the first real send

17. **W1093** (ready) Warn on a card blocked on a card that is done in another repo
   - next: A nemik-check shape: a blocked card whose blocked_on names `<repo>`:W`<n>` that is done in that repo's queue

     the host's ledger had none from 2026-10-25T18:44Z because its ticks touched only the liveness stamp

24. **W1179** (ready) Codon in larger steps: thin entry points, one dispatcher and one I/O adapter
   - next: CORRECTION 2026-10-27 to the previous note on datetime.UTC (tested, not inferred): the pinned Codon's
     datetime module has NEITHER UTC NOR timezone ('cannot import name UTC from datetime'; 'cannot import name timezone
     from datetime', probed with codon run on two three-line files). So there is no ruff-versus-Codon choice to make:
     ruff's UP017 would flag timezone.utc (confirmed) but Codon cannot import timezone either. The four modules that
     import UTC (alarm, witwait, queuerows, residue) use timezone-aware datetimes, which the Codon datetime cannot
     express; a Codon-compatible form means those modules stop handling zoned datetimes (take and return epoch seconds
     or ISO text, with the zone handled in a non-census caller), an architectural change per module, not an import swap.
     Everything else in the previous note stands: the ',' cluster is the bare '*,' marker; pacerun left the baseline in
     W1190 (frozenset default became a list constant; Codon cannot read tuple[str, ...]); the tally by first error (18
     argparse, 10 ',', 9 rdflib, 8 tomllib, 7 class decorator, 4 importlib.resources, 4 collections.abc, singletons) and
     calendars and rank aborting the compiler (rc -6) unexplained

     vec; _a), mypy 5248 to 4928 and ruff 2595 to 2569, because one untyped helper turned every caller's result into

- **nemik:R38** (instructions) round 34: 664 (89b244c) landed 13:25:44Z in 6 min 57 s; gate empty 13:25:44Z to the 665

- **nemik:R40** (gate) round 36: 667 (c0a10be, 8886074), 668 (c58dc3a), 669 (713ecfe) landed; 670 (W1014 slice 4)
  inflight about 16:49:43Z; ruff 1880 to 1859; gaps 11 min 38 s, 14 min 40 s, 16 min 32 s; 668 first submit refused for

  partly met. The gaps are still over 10 min, and what fills them is the census (334 to 366 s) plus edit time; the
  census starts after the edit. Separately, the working-mark refusal came back (fourth instance of W1219): a card left
  'working' after its landing. The cure then surfaced a second rule: a landed card needs a witness, and the repin
  deploys nothing, so the closing state was misjudged twice.; 4 finding(s)
- **nemik:R41** (instructions) round 37: 670 (69155cf) and 671 (7bc4611) landed, 672 (W1014 slice 6) inflight about
  17:48:08Z, ruff 1859 to 1831, gaps 16 min 32 s, 15 min 46 s, 16 min 42 s (R40-F2 not met, 3 of 3), W1014 has about 14
  unread D205 sites: root The gap is a fixed sequence run after the landing: tick-open, G0, edits, measure, census (282
  to 328 s), stack writing. Edits in one batch right after the landing do not shrink it, because every step still
  follows the landing. The one case that cut the gap (656 gating while the E501 batch was built) did the edit and the
  census during the previous gate. Doing the next slice's edit, census and G0 while the current stack gates removes the
  sequence from the empty interval.; 4 finding(s)
- **nemik:R42** (instructions) round 38: 672 (1f47caa) landed 17:59:10Z, 673 (W1014 slice 7, ruff 1831 to 1819, census
  435.08 s) inflight about 18:14:25Z, gap 15 min 11 s, slices 7 and 8 both edit serve.py so slice 8 must wait for 673,
  stale pace card W1277 and class emitter card W1276 minted: root The gap target was missed a fourth round. R41-F1 was
  partly impossible (slice 8 shares serve.py with the in-gate slice), which is a real constraint the finding did not
  account for: overlapping files serialize the pipeline. A gap that cannot be removed by working ahead is removed by
  choosing slices that do not share files, so that the next slice is independent of the one gating. Separately a status
  statement ('a few minutes') was sent to a peer without a reading and corrected after.; 4 finding(s)

  after 674: root The re-cut (R42-F1) merged two slices into one because the file was free, which was right, but it made
  the stack bigger and the gap the longest of six: one E501 at measure forced a census restart on a loaded host (543.79
  s). The census restart is a step that follows a late measure. Measuring E501 and ruff before the census starts would
  avoid restarting it. The reads of state were correct this round: the reflog and --in-gate for github-42, systemctl for
  the pace unit.; 4 finding(s)

- **nemik:R62** (gate) round 58: 697 (ebb44c9, 47c1f87) landed pushed 06:18:16Z, ls-remote read back, ruff 1660 to 1650;
  ratchetseries.py read (the only dedupe is by sample time, not by commit, stack or verdict); 698 (W1279 slice 6, 15
  Returns sections, ruff 1650 to 1635) inflight 06:19:53Z; gap 1 min 34 s; W1282 waits a fifth round: root The read of
  ratchetseries.py changes the debt arithmetic: the pace's window counts one sample per gate run, deduped only by
  timestamp, so refused gates, re-gates and runs of stacks that do not touch a ratchet (flat samples) each occupy a
  slot. That is why ruff-neutral landings dilute the window, and why a refusal costs a slot as well as a gate cycle. The
  unread half, ratchetpace.py (how fell and owes come from the 10 samples), decides whether the 73 can be cleared, and
  neither the session nor mtools has read it. The process rules (landing 8 of 8, starting 5 of 5) hold; the open risk is
  that the plan is built on a half-read producer.; 4 finding(s)
- **nemik:R63** (gate) round 59: 698 (04326df) landed pushed 06:47:10Z after 27 min 20 s, the longest gate of the night,
  ls-remote read back; ratchetpace.py read (window_fall is end minus start; requirement only rises, seeded from the

  sections, ruff 1635 to 1617) inflight 06:51:46Z with a stated hypothesis about gate time; gap 4 min 27 s: root The
  producer is now read: fell is the value ten samples back minus the value now, so a rise inside the window counts
  against it, flat samples are slots, and the requirement is min(max(prior, seed, 1), now) with the seed the median fall
  of earlier windows (needs 3), so it only rises. The formula held against two printed paces (fell 52 = 1721-1669, fell
  71 = 1721-1650). The one wrong claim was a prediction about timing: a stack's sample enters the series 5 to 6 minutes
  into its gate, not at its start, which the session stated as wrong before it was asked and corrected on W1179. The
  longer-term implication is that falling the ruff value by a large step is what matters (end minus start).; 4
  finding(s)
